## Assistant (Build · GLM-5.2 · 104.7s)

The drop-order changes are safe — `OwnedPtr::drop` just calls `cpp_delete` to free C++ memory; there are no lock releases or inter-dependencies between the temporaries and locals. The new drop order (temporaries dropped before locals) is harmless here. Let me verify the build:

## Assistant (Build · GLM-5.2 · 7.2s)

The macro itself uses `#[export_name = "init-model"]` which needs to be `#[unsafe(export_name = "init-model")]` in edition 2024. The `cargo fix` updated the `export!` macro's usage at line 66 but not this custom macro. Let me fix it:

**Tool: edit**

```json
{
  "filePath": "/Users/sethml/src/opencascade-rs/crates/model-api/src/wasm.rs",
  "newString": "        #[unsafe(export_name = \"init-model\")]",
  "oldString": "        #[export_name = \"init-model\"]"
}
```

## Assistant (Build · GLM-5.2 · 13.7s)

Everything builds and all tests pass. Here's a summary of what was done:

## Summary

Upgraded all 9 crates from edition 2021 to edition 2024 following the official migration process:

1. **`cargo fix --edition`** (while still on 2021) — auto-applied thousands of fixes across the generated FFI bindings and source files:
   - Wrapped unsafe function calls in `unsafe` blocks inside `unsafe fn` bodies (the "unsafe-op-in-unsafe-fn" migration) across ~250 generated files
   - `crates/opencascade-sys/build.rs`: wrapped `std::env::set_var` in `unsafe` (now unsafe in 2024)
   - `crates/opencascade-sys/src/exception.rs`: `#[no_mangle]` → `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]`
   - `examples/tests/golden.rs`: `expr` → `expr_2021` in macros (preserving 2021 capture behavior)

2. **Updated all `Cargo.toml` files** to `edition = "2024"`

3. **Manual fixes** for things `cargo fix` couldn't auto-detect:
   - `crates/model-api/src/wasm.rs`: `#[export_name = "init-model"]` → `#[unsafe(export_name = "init-model")]` inside the `register_model!` macro (the macro-generated code needed the unsafe attribute wrapper, which only surfaced when the `wasm-example` crate compiled)
   - Improved the `set_var` safety comment

4. **Reviewed the drop-order warnings** — the edition 2024 tail-expression drop order change is safe here, since `OwnedPtr::drop` just calls `cpp_delete` to free C++ memory with no inter-dependencies between temporaries and locals.

### Verification
- `cargo build` — passes
- `cargo test --workspace --exclude opencascade-binding-generator` — 33 tests pass (4 ignored), 0 failures
- `scripts/run-binding-generator-tests.sh` — 17 tests pass, 0 failures

Note: the generated code in `crates/opencascade-sys/generated/` was modified by `cargo fix`. Per AGENTS.md, generated code is normally regenerated rather than hand-edited, but here the edition migration lints are a one-time mechanical transformation that `cargo fix` applies correctly. You may want to also update the binding generator to emit edition-2024-compatible code (with the `unsafe` blocks inline) so future regenerations stay clean — but that's optional since `cargo fix --edition` can always be re-run.

Let me first look at the generator's codegen to understand how it emits the unsafe blocks.

**Tool: bash**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "command": "cat scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh"
}
```

**Output:**
```
#!/bin/bash
# Regenerate OpenCASCADE bindings
#
# This script regenerates the FFI bindings for opencascade-sys.
# Run from the repository root.
#
# Usage:
#   ./scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh
#
# Requirements:
#   - Xcode command line tools (for libclang)
#   - OCCT headers built in target/OCCT/include

set -euo pipefail

# Repository root (script assumes it's run from repo root)
REPO_ROOT="${REPO_ROOT:-$(pwd)}"
cd "$REPO_ROOT"

# Paths
OCCT_INCLUDE="$REPO_ROOT/target/OCCT/include"
OUTPUT_DIR="$REPO_ROOT/crates/opencascade-sys/generated"
CONFIG_FILE="$REPO_ROOT/crates/opencascade-sys/bindings.toml"

# Check prerequisites
if [[ ! -d "$OCCT_INCLUDE" ]]; then
    echo "Error: OCCT include directory not found at $OCCT_INCLUDE"
    echo "Run 'cargo build -p occt-sys' first to build OCCT"
    exit 1
fi

if [[ ! -f "$CONFIG_FILE" ]]; then
    echo "Error: Config file not found at $CONFIG_FILE"
    exit 1
fi

# Build the generator in release mode to avoid a UB crash in the `clang` crate
# (v2.0.0) that only manifests in debug builds on Rust >= 1.78. See
# crates/opencascade-binding-generator/Cargo.toml for details.
echo "Building binding generator..."
cargo build --release -p opencascade-binding-generator

echo "Generating bindings from $CONFIG_FILE..."

# Clean generated directory
echo "Cleaning $OUTPUT_DIR..."
rm -f "$OUTPUT_DIR"/*.rs "$OUTPUT_DIR"/*.hxx "$OUTPUT_DIR"/*.cpp

# Set library path for libclang on macOS
if [[ "$(uname)" == "Darwin" ]]; then
    XCODE_TOOLCHAIN="$(xcode-select -p)/Toolchains/XcodeDefault.xctoolchain/usr/lib"
    export DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH="$XCODE_TOOLCHAIN:${DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH:-}"
fi

# Run the generator
"$REPO_ROOT/target/release/occt-bindgen" \
    --config "$CONFIG_FILE" \
    -I "$OCCT_INCLUDE" \
    -o "$OUTPUT_DIR" \
    "$@"

echo ""
echo "Bindings generated in $OUTPUT_DIR"

```

1: // Manual bindings for C++ iostream global objects (std::cout, std::cerr, etc.)
2: //
3: // These provide access to C++ standard output/error streams, which can be

5: // DumpJson, Print).

7: // This file is include!()'d into the generated standard.rs module, which

17: /// Returns a mutable reference to `std::cout` (C++ standard output stream).
18: ///
19: /// # Safety
20: ///
21: /// The returned reference points to the global C++ `std::cout` object.
22: /// Concurrent writes from multiple threads are safe but may result in
23: /// interleaved output. The reference is valid for the entire program lifetime.
24: ///
25: /// # Example
26: ///
27: /// ```no_run
28: /// let cout = opencascade_sys::standard::cout();
29: /// // Pass to OCCT methods that take &mut OStream:
30: /// // some_shape.dump_json(cout, 0);
31: /// ```
32: pub fn cout() -> &'static mut OStream {
33:     unsafe { &mut *iostream_cout() }
34: }
35: 
36: /// Returns a mutable reference to `std::cerr` (C++ standard error stream).
37: ///
38: /// # Safety
39: ///
40: /// Same safety requirements as [`cout()`].
41: pub fn cerr() -> &'static mut OStream {
42:     unsafe { &mut *iostream_cerr() }
43: }
44: 
45: /// Returns a mutable reference to `std::clog` (C++ standard log stream).
46: ///
47: /// # Safety
48: ///
49: /// Same safety requirements as [`cout()`].
50: pub fn clog() -> &'static mut OStream {
51:     unsafe { &mut *iostream_clog() }
52: }
53: 
54: /// Returns a mutable reference to `std::cin` (C++ standard input stream).
55: ///
56: /// # Safety
57: ///
58: /// Same safety requirements as [`cout()`].

61: }

## Assistant (Build · GLM-5.2 · 16.4s)

All done. Here's a summary of the generator changes needed for edition 2024:

## Generator Changes

Three patterns in the binding generator needed updating for edition 2024 compatibility:

1. **`unsafe extern "C" { }`** — Edition 2024 requires `extern` blocks to be marked `unsafe`. Fixed in `codegen/rust.rs` at two locations (toolkit FFI blocks and extra-handle FFI blocks).

2. **`unsafe { }` blocks inside `unsafe fn cpp_delete`/`cpp_copy`** — Edition 2024 no longer makes `unsafe fn` bodies implicitly unsafe (the "unsafe-op-in-unsafe-fn" change). Fixed in 10 locations across `codegen/rust.rs` and `codegen/bindings.rs`:
   - `CppDeletable::cpp_delete` impls (nested types, extra handles, collections, iterators, POD structs, regular classes, handle types)
   - `CppCopyable::cpp_copy` impls (extra handles, copyable classes, handle types)

3. **`unsafe { }` blocks in convenience constructors** — When an `unsafe fn` convenience constructor delegates to another `unsafe fn` full-arg constructor, the call now needs an explicit `unsafe { }` block. Fixed in `codegen/bindings.rs`.

## Verification

- `scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh` — regenerated 321 modules
- `cargo build` — passes
- `scripts/run-binding-generator-tests.sh` — 17 tests pass, 0 failures
- `cargo test --workspace --exclude opencascade-binding-generator` — 33 tests pass, 0 failures

```
-## File Editing
-
-When reading files for editing, use `#tool:hashlineRead` instead of the
-built-in file read tool. It returns lines tagged with content hashes in the
-format `{lineNumber}:{hash}|{content}`.
-
-When editing files, use `#tool:hashlineEdit` instead of string-replace tools.
-Reference lines by their `{line}:{hash}` pairs from the read output. This
-avoids needing to reproduce existing file content and prevents edits to stale
-files.
-
-Example workflow:
-1. Read: `hashline_read({filePath: "src/app.ts", startLine: 1, endLine: 20})`
-   Returns: `1:qk|import React...`
-2. Edit: `hashline_edit({edits: [{filePath: "src/app.ts", lineHashes: "4:mp", content: "  return <div>Hello</div>;"}]})`
-
-Operations:
-- **Replace**: set `lineHashes` to all lines being replaced, `content` to new text
-- **Insert after**: set `insertAfter: true`, `lineHashes` to anchor line
-- **Delete**: set `content` to empty string
-- Multiple edits can be batched in one call across files
```
